Semenov et al. 2026 · Bioelectrochemistry

Sub-microsecond optical measurements of cell membrane charging and lesioning by pulsed electric fields

✓ Verified by an independent agent

Iurii Semenov, Giedrė Šilkūnienė, Mantas Šilkūnas, Olga N. Pakhomova, Joel N. Bixler, Bennett L. Ibey, Peter Kochunov, Andrei G. Pakhomov

doi:10.1016/j.bioelechem.2026.109283 · paper key semenov-2026

  • ◐1 partially reproduced
  • ?9 not reproduced
  • ⊘42 blocked

On DANDI:001515: Almost none of the paper's findings could be tested here, and none was shown to fail. The dandiset holds FluoVolt time courses of single 1-us pulses, not the paper's Di-8-ANEPPS and FluoVolt escalation sets, so the polarization pattern, the loss of pole charging at electroporating fields, and the Gd3+, calcium and two-pulse resealing results could not be tested. The one claim needing no recordings, the effect of membrane conductivity on charging, partly held: the 100-fold value matches, but a 10-fold rise gives 1.3% where the paper says about 2%.

On DANDI:001516: Almost none of the paper's findings could be tested in this data, and the one that could be tested did not match the paper's text. The dandiset holds a FluoVolt two-pulse experiment the paper does not report, not the Di-8-ANEPPS recordings behind its loss of pole charging at high fields, the Gd3+ extension of anodic charging, the calcium entry or the membrane recovery after a conditioning pulse. Those findings could not be tested for lack of data. The only claim needing no recordings, that a modest rise in membrane conductivity cuts charging by about 2%, gave 1.33% from the paper's own equation and constants. The larger-rise value agreed.

On DANDI:001517: The pole dimming at high fields, the Di-8-ANEPPS rise up to 570 V/cm and the within-set decline from 1000 V/cm held, but the dye saturation limits and the equator following the cosine law did not. FluoVolt peaked at 49% cathodic dF against the paper's 66%. The likeliest reason is the background region, which the paper does not place and which shifts pole dF up to threefold. Gd3+, calcium, 0.3-us and two-pulse findings could not be tested because those recordings are not in the dataset. Charging below linear scaling held only with generator volts matched in order to the paper's V/cm.

On DANDI:001518: The paragraph is below, ready to save. Most of the paper's main findings did not hold in this data or could not be tested. The Gd3+ effects, calcium responses, two-pulse recovery and 0.3-us pulses could not be tested because the dandiset lacks those experiments. The dye response ceilings (FluoVolt peaked at 36% against 71%), the dye calibration, the pole dimming and the cosine behaviour near the equator did not reproduce. These tests assume a volts to V/cm conversion that the files and paper do not give, though the ceilings fail at every conversion tried. Only the within-set decline of Di-8-ANEPPS response reproduced, mostly as a drift present at every field strength.

On DANDI:001521: Most of the paper's main findings, the optical measurements of membrane charging and pole leakage with voltage-sensitive dyes, could not be tested, because this dandiset holds only Fluo-4 calcium imaging. Of the calcium findings, clear Ca2+ entry after a single pulse at 1000 and 1200 V/cm held, though the effect is small. The signal staying elevated through 5 minutes did not hold, because unpulsed cells lose about 12% of their signal, which the paper's figure does not show. The strong response in 28% of cells at 780 V/cm with pulse trains did not reproduce either. The records give no way to match the paper's cell counts, since the cells were recorded repeatedly. A minor conductivity calculation matched only in part.

On DANDI:001525: The paper's membrane recovery findings mostly hold in this data, which covers only its two-pulse experiment, so most other findings could not be tested. Test-pulse charging stays cosine-shaped and unchanged after mild conditioning pulses and falls after the strongest ones, as reported. The fall differs in size from the paper's at the cathodic pole, and at the anodic pole it is not significant after the weaker of the two strongest pulses. Full resealing after mild pulses and residual leak after strong ones hold, but the test pulse also finds a pole difference after the strongest pulse. These results depend on an automatic membrane trace, and the dye calibration, escalation, Gd3+ and calcium findings are absent from the dataset.

On DANDI:001526: Few of the paper's findings could be tested, and none held up cleanly. The dandiset holds only the Di-8-ANEPPS alternating-field and short-pulse experiments, so the FluoVolt, Gd3+, calcium and two-pulse recovery findings could not be tested. Stable responses before electroporation did not reproduce, and the lasting drop afterwards was larger than reported, both depending on an undescribed background subtraction and on which cells the paper used. The fall in polarization within a set at the electroporating field held only under one background choice made after seeing results. The maximum polarization with short pulses reached 34.9% against the reported 53%.

Summary written by the agent that recorded the attempt, from its claims and the agents’ reports. A failure to reproduce may be a failure of the agent.

◐Partially reproduced1

  • ◐Partially reproducedFigure 5B; Results 3.5, paragraph 3

    By Eq. (2), the effective membrane conductivity barely affects charging while it stays far below cytosol and medium conductivities; a 10-fold rise reduces IMP by about 2% and a 100-fold rise by 12%.

    its 10-fold increase would go unnoticed, yielding just a ~2% IMP reduction, and a 100-fold increase would reduce IMP by just 12%.

    Figure 5B; Results 3.5, paragraph 3

    Eq. (2) with the constants of Methods 2.3 gives an IMP reduction of 1.33% (reading a, 1 - f/1.5) or 1.20% (reading b, relative to f at 3e-7 S/m) for a 10-fold rise of membrane conductivity, outside 2 +- 0.2%, and 11.87% (a) or 11.75% (b) for a 100-fold rise, inside 12 +- 1.2%. Both readings give the same result. The qualitative part of the statement holds, as a 1000-fold rise is needed to reduce IMP by more than half (57.4%). In a grid of radii 5 to 15 um and membrane thicknesses 3 to 7 nm (a variant, not the criterion), no combination puts both values within tolerance under reading (a); R = 8 um with d = 3 nm gives 2.19% and 18.3%. The computation uses no recordings.

    How the test differed from the paper’s method

    • None in method; Eq. (2) and its constants are those of Methods 2.3, and both readings of the criterion were run.

  • ⊘BlockedFigure 1, 43 to 570 V/cm; Results 3.1, paragraph 1· Data missing from the dataset

    Below and near the electroporation threshold (43 to 570 V/cm, 1-us pulses), the membrane depolarizes at the cathode-facing pole and hyperpolarizes at the anode-facing pole, polarization decreases from the poles towards the equator, and it scales proportionally with field strength, as the Schwan equation describes.

    Electric pulses below and near the electroporation threshold produce a well-known spatial pattern of membrane polarization, such as in Fig. 1, 43–570 V/cm. The membrane depolarizes at the cathode-facing membrane pole and hyperpolarizes at the opposite, anode-facing pole. Polarization gradually decreases from the poles towards the cell equator, and scales up proportionally with the electric field strength

    Figure 1, 43 to 570 V/cm; Results 3.1, paragraph 1

    The dandiset holds 292 files of FluoVolt strobe-imaging time courses (70 frames each, one 1-us pulse amplitude per subject, 20 to 100 V across electrodes 1.00 mm apart, frame delays not recorded). The claim needs Di-8-ANEPPS recordings; every file is FluoVolt. It also needs the paper's escalation sets of 40 baseline and 60 pulse images at 900 ns, which no file has. Dandisets 001516, 001517 and 001518 also name this paper and may hold these data; they are separate targets of the registry and were not read here.

    With Di-8-ANEPPS and 1-us pulses, polarization grows with field strength up to 570 V/cm; at 780 V/cm the cathodic pole stops increasing and dims, and at 1000 to 1400 V/cm the poles show reduced or absent polarization while regions nearer the equator stay polarized and may polarize more than the poles.

    At a higher electric field strength of 780 V/cm, polarization at the cathodic pole does not further increase but dims out, indicating a less efficient charging of the partially permeabilized membrane.   • ⊘BlockedFigure 2A; Results 3.2, paragraph 2· Data missing from the dataset

    With FluoVolt, pole dF rose with field strength until it saturated at 66% (255 mV) at 360 V/cm at the cathodic pole and -83% (-320 mV) at 570 V/cm at the anodic pole; stronger fields did not increase the anodic response and decreased the cathodic one.

    ΔF increased proportionally to the electric field strength until reaching apparent saturation at 66% ΔF (255 mV IMP) at 360 V/cm at the cathodic pole, and −83% ΔF (−320 mV IMP) at 570 V/cm at the anodic pole. Further escalation of the electric field strength did not increase the dye response at the anodic pole and even decreased it at the cathodic pole.

    Figure 2A; Results 3.2, paragraph 2

    The dandiset holds 292 files of FluoVolt strobe-imaging time courses (70 frames each, one 1-us pulse amplitude per subject, 20 to 100 V across electrodes 1.00 mm apart, frame delays not recorded). The claim needs the FluoVolt experiments of the paper, sets of 40 baseline and 60 pulse images at 900 ns into 1-us pulses with the field escalated from 43 to 1400 V/cm within each cell (n = 4); no file has that structure, and no cell is exposed to more than one amplitude. Using the dandiset's time courses in their place would also need the delay of each frame (to find the 900-ns frame) and the field strength in V/cm (the paper's comes from a Sim4Life model of 1.6-mm electrodes), neither of which the files record, so this is also metadata_insufficient. Dandisets 001516, 001517 and 001518 also name this paper and may hold these data; they are separate targets of the registry and were not read here.

  • ?Not reproducedFigure 5B; Results 3.5, paragraph 3

    By Eq. (2), the effective membrane conductivity barely affects charging while it stays far below cytosol and medium conductivities; a 10-fold rise reduces IMP by about 2% and a 100-fold rise by 12%.

    its 10-fold increase would go unnoticed, yielding just a ~2% IMP reduction, and a 100-fold increase would reduce IMP by just 12%.

    Figure 5B; Results 3.5, paragraph 3

    Eq. (2) with the constants of Methods 2.3 gives f = 1.49798 for the intact membrane. A 10-fold rise in effective conductivity reduces IMP by 1.33% under reading (a), 1 - f/1.5, and by 1.20% under reading (b), relative to the intact f, both outside 2 +- 0.2%. A 100-fold rise gives 11.87% and 11.75%, within 12 +- 1.2%. A 2% reduction needs a 15.2-fold (a) or 16.2-fold (b) rise. With other radii and membrane thicknesses (5 to 12 um, 4 to 7 nm; a variant, not the criterion) the 10-fold reduction is 0.60 to 2.46%, within 2 +- 0.2% only for 10 um with 4 nm (2.06%) or 12 um with 5 nm (1.98%). The claim uses no recordings, so the outcome says nothing about the dataset. The notebook prints the Eq. (2) curve at 1 to 100000-fold (measured IMP 98.67% of IMPC at 10-fold, 88.13% at 100-fold, 42.62% at 1000-fold, 6.91% at 10000-fold) for comparison with the curve of Fig. 5B; it does not read the figure.

Reproducing Semenov et al. (2026) from dandiset 001517 16

Notebook reproduce_semenov_2026.ipynb

✓Reproduced5

  • ✓ReproducedFigure 1 and its legend; Results 3.1, paragraph 1rests on an assumption

    With Di-8-ANEPPS and 1-us pulses, polarization grows with field strength up to 570 V/cm; at 780 V/cm the cathodic pole stops increasing and dims, and at 1000 to 1400 V/cm the poles show reduced or absent polarization while regions nearer the equator stay polarized and may polarize more than the poles.

    At a higher electric field strength of 780 V/cm, polarization at the cathodic pole does not further increase but dims out, indicating a less efficient charging of the partially permeabilized membrane. Still stronger electric fields produce a distinct pattern of reduced or absent polarization at the poles ... while regions closer to the equator remain relatively intact and may polarize more than the poles

    Figure 1 and its legend; Results 3.1, paragraph 1

    Under every background reading the cathodic pole is lower at 780 than at 570 V/cm (far 38.6 against 41.7%; near 65.0 against 67.9; none 15.2 against 16.0), and at 1400 V/cm the largest mean dF of the cathode-facing hemisphere lies 65 degrees from the pole (far 21.7% at -65 degrees against 7.5% at the pole; near and none also at -65). Both conditions hold at every band of the background sweep and with the local backgrounds. In each of the 8 cells the 1400 V/cm maximum lies 43 to 76 degrees from the pole. Five-cell subsets passing 56 of 56 (far), 45 (near), 51 (none). The Fig. 1 cell is not identified; the test uses the Fig. 4B cells as the criterion says.

    How the test differed from the paper’s method

    • Field strengths are matched in order to the generator volts the files give (25 V as 43 V/cm, 50 as 150, 70 as 230, 100 as 360, 150 as 570, 200 as 780, 250 as 1000, 300 as 1200, 350 as 1400), an assumption. The paper names nine field strengths for its 1-us escalations and the files nine amplitudes, but the two are not proportional, and below electroporation the data fit a proportional response about as well on the volts as on the paper's V/cm. The first step supports the matching: from 25 to 50 V the mean pole |dF| triples (FluoVolt 3.05, Di-8-ANEPPS 2.90), against 3.49 for the paper's 43 to 150 V/cm and 2.00 for proportional volts. A variant with the field proportional to the volts (4 V/cm per V) is shown for every claim that uses the field strength as a number.
    • Background is subtracted from ROIs 30 to 36 pixels (8 to 10 um) outside the membrane, 20 degrees wide, near each pole (the far reading), an assumption. The paper does not state a background method for the laser images; its Methods follow reference [20] ('Most methods were identical to those described in detail recently [20]'), which places background ROIs 'near the poles outside of the cell' with no distance. The data check shows the membrane's glow is still a third of its excess 2 um out and a few percent 8 um out. The near (2 to 4 um) and no-background readings are shown and change dF by up to a factor of three. After verification round 1 every claim also shows a sweep of 6-pixel bands from 8 to 44 pixels out and a per-pole (local) background.
    • The perimeter trace (6 pixels wide, Methods 2.3) is found automatically as the peak of intensity on 360 rays of each set's baseline image, with polar angles from the cell centre; the paper drew it by hand in MetaMorph and resampled by perimeter length. Sets whose outline radius departs from the cell's median by more than 15% are traced again within 8 pixels of the cell's median centre and 4 pixels of its median radius; this re-traced FluoVolt c2 at 300 V (outline on a neighbouring cell) and Di-8-ANEPPS c7 at 300 and 350 V (outline on a body inside the cell), found in the outline overlays added after verification round 1.
    • Frames without a laser flash, which the files do not flag (frame 0 of 137 of 141 sets and 290 others), are left out of every average; the paper does not mention them. Keeping them is shown as a variant.
    • The field direction is not stored. The cathode-facing pole is placed where dF is positive (Methods 2.3, emission increased in the cathode-facing hemisphere), on one axis for all sets estimated from the 50 to 100 V sets (per-set median deviation 1.6 degrees).
    • All eight Di-8-ANEPPS escalation cells (c7 to c14) are used where the paper reports n = 5 without naming them; the criterion is also run on each of the 56 five-cell subsets.
    • Di-8-ANEPPS c13 has two 150 V sets; the first, in escalation order, is used.
  • ✓ReproducedFigure 2B; Results 3.2, paragraph 3rests on an assumption

    • Field strengths are matched in order to the generator volts the files give (25 V as 43 V/cm, 50 as 150, 70 as 230, 100 as 360, 150 as 570, 200 as 780, 250 as 1000, 300 as 1200, 350 as 1400), an assumption.     • Background is subtracted from ROIs 30 to 36 pixels (8 to 10 um) outside the membrane, 20 degrees wide, near each pole (the far reading), an assumption.     • Frames without a laser flash, which the files do not flag (frame 0 of 137 of 141 sets and 290 others), are left out of every average; the paper does not mention them. Keeping them is shown as a variant.
    • The field direction is not stored. The cathode-facing pole is placed where dF is positive (Methods 2.3, emission increased in the cathode-facing hemisphere), on one axis for all sets estimated from the 50 to 100 V sets (per-set median deviation 1.6 degrees).

    • Di-8-ANEPPS c13 has two 150 V sets; the first, in escalation order, is used.

    Far background, cathodic |dF| from start to end of each set, reading (a) 40.1 to 35.8, 40.8 to 22.4, 32.2 to 17.4, 16.4 to 8.7% at 780 to 1400 V/cm; reading (b) 41.1 to 34.3, 37.0 to 25.2, 25.5 to 15.3, 15.6 to 8.7% (paired p 0.017, 0.001, 0.002, 0.092). Both readings decline in every set under every band of the sweep, without background and with the near background; with the near local background reading (a) fails. Five-cell subsets passing 44 (a) and 52 (b) of 56. The anodic variant does not decline at 780 V/cm. Bleaching control (after verification round 1): below electroporation the cathodic dF also falls through each set (reading b, -3.5 to -4.6% at 230 to 570 V/cm, common-mode change of both poles -1.7 to -3.4%), so the fall at 780 V/cm (-6.8%, -3.3% net of the common mode against -2.6% at 570) is about the size of the bleaching drift; the falls at 1000 to 1400 V/cm (-9.5 to -11.8%, net -6.0 to -7.5%) exceed it. The criterion holds as written; the part at 780 V/cm cannot be told from bleaching.

    How the test differed from the paper’s method

    • Field strengths are matched in order to the generator volts the files give (25 V as 43 V/cm, 50 as 150, 70 as 230, 100 as 360, 150 as 570, 200 as 780, 250 as 1000, 300 as 1200, 350 as 1400), an assumption. The paper names nine field strengths for its 1-us escalations and the files nine amplitudes, but the two are not proportional, and below electroporation the data fit a proportional response about as well on the volts as on the paper's V/cm. The first step supports the matching: from 25 to 50 V the mean pole |dF| triples (FluoVolt 3.05, Di-8-ANEPPS 2.90), against 3.49 for the paper's 43 to 150 V/cm and 2.00 for proportional volts. A variant with the field proportional to the volts (4 V/cm per V) is shown for every claim that uses the field strength as a number.
    • Background is subtracted from ROIs 30 to 36 pixels (8 to 10 um) outside the membrane, 20 degrees wide, near each pole (the far reading), an assumption. The paper does not state a background method for the laser images; its Methods follow reference [20] ('Most methods were identical to those described in detail recently [20]'), which places background ROIs 'near the poles outside of the cell' with no distance. The data check shows the membrane's glow is still a third of its excess 2 um out and a few percent 8 um out. The near (2 to 4 um) and no-background readings are shown and change dF by up to a factor of three. After verification round 1 every claim also shows a sweep of 6-pixel bands from 8 to 44 pixels out and a per-pole (local) background.
    • The perimeter trace (6 pixels wide, Methods 2.3) is found automatically as the peak of intensity on 360 rays of each set's baseline image, with polar angles from the cell centre; the paper drew it by hand in MetaMorph and resampled by perimeter length. Sets whose outline radius departs from the cell's median by more than 15% are traced again within 8 pixels of the cell's median centre and 4 pixels of its median radius; this re-traced FluoVolt c2 at 300 V (outline on a neighbouring cell) and Di-8-ANEPPS c7 at 300 and 350 V (outline on a body inside the cell), found in the outline overlays added after verification round 1.
    • Frames without a laser flash, which the files do not flag (frame 0 of 137 of 141 sets and 290 others), are left out of every average; the paper does not mention them. Keeping them is shown as a variant.
    • The field direction is not stored. The cathode-facing pole is placed where dF is positive (Methods 2.3, emission increased in the cathode-facing hemisphere), on one axis for all sets estimated from the 50 to 100 V sets (per-set median deviation 1.6 degrees).
    • All eight Di-8-ANEPPS escalation cells (c7 to c14) are used where the paper reports n = 5 without naming them; the criterion is also run on each of the 56 five-cell subsets.
    • Di-8-ANEPPS c13 has two 150 V sets; the first, in escalation order, is used.

    By Eq. (2), the effective membrane conductivity barely affects charging while it stays far below cytosol and medium conductivities; a 10-fold rise reduces IMP by about 2% and a 100-fold rise by 12%.

    Figure 5B; Results 3.5, paragraph 3

    Eq. (2) with the Methods constants gives f = 1.498 at 3e-7 S/m. A 100-fold rise reduces IMP by 11.87% (reading a, 1 - f/1.5) or 11.75% (b), within 12 +- 1.2; a 10-fold rise by 1.33% (a) or 1.20% (b), outside 2 +- 0.2. Under Eq. (2) a 2% reduction needs a 15.2-fold rise. The first part of the statement (little effect while the conductivity is far below the cytosol and medium) holds (99.6% of 1.5 at 1e-6 S/m).

    Figure 3 and legend; Results 3.3, paragraph 1

    Di-8-ANEPPS departs from the shared line on the cathodic side under every smooth and background (far, 2709 of 5418 LOESS points from 150 to 500 mV, 5 of 14 bins; smooth over line down to 0.73). Without the 43 V/cm sets, whose cathodic values are negative (fig2b-di8-bleaching), the slopes are 10.15 (anodic) and 4.59 (cathodic), so those points account for only part of the asymmetry (output added after verification round 2). FluoVolt departs at every |IMPC| from 220 mV under LOESS 0.5 and 0.75 but not under LOESS 0.3 or 50-mV bins (far); under the near background it does under all four. With the 8-um pixel (after verification round 1) FluoVolt passes under every smooth and background but Di-8-ANEPPS still departs under every smooth (smooth over line down to 0.59 to 0.85); with the field proportional to the volts both parts fail.

    How the test differed from the paper’s method

    • Field strengths are matched in order to the generator volts the files give (25 V as 43 V/cm, 50 as 150, 70 as 230, 100 as 360, 150 as 570, 200 as 780, 250 as 1000, 300 as 1200, 350 as 1400), an assumption. The paper names nine field strengths for its 1-us escalations and the files nine amplitudes, but the two are not proportional, and below electroporation the data fit a proportional response about as well on the volts as on the paper's V/cm. The first step supports the matching: from 25 to 50 V the mean pole |dF| triples (FluoVolt 3.05, Di-8-ANEPPS 2.90), against 3.49 for the paper's 43 to 150 V/cm and 2.00 for proportional volts. A variant with the field proportional to the volts (4 V/cm per V) is shown for every claim that uses the field strength as a number.
    • Background is subtracted from ROIs 30 to 36 pixels (8 to 10 um) outside the membrane, 20 degrees wide, near each pole (the far reading), an assumption. The paper does not state a background method for the laser images; its Methods follow reference [20] ('Most methods were identical to those described in detail recently [20]'), which places background ROIs 'near the poles outside of the cell' with no distance. The data check shows the membrane's glow is still a third of its excess 2 um out and a few percent 8 um out. The near (2 to 4 um) and no-background readings are shown and change dF by up to a factor of three. After verification round 1 every claim also shows a sweep of 6-pixel bands from 8 to 44 pixels out and a per-pole (local) background.
    • The perimeter trace (6 pixels wide, Methods 2.3) is found automatically as the peak of intensity on 360 rays of each set's baseline image, with polar angles from the cell centre; the paper drew it by hand in MetaMorph and resampled by perimeter length. Sets whose outline radius departs from the cell's median by more than 15% are traced again within 8 pixels of the cell's median centre and 4 pixels of its median radius; this re-traced FluoVolt c2 at 300 V (outline on a neighbouring cell) and Di-8-ANEPPS c7 at 300 and 350 V (outline on a body inside the cell), found in the outline overlays added after verification round 1.
    • Frames without a laser flash, which the files do not flag (frame 0 of 137 of 141 sets and 290 others), are left out of every average; the paper does not mention them. Keeping them is shown as a variant.
    • The field direction is not stored. The cathode-facing pole is placed where dF is positive (Methods 2.3, emission increased in the cathode-facing hemisphere), on one axis for all sets estimated from the 50 to 100 V sets (per-set median deviation 1.6 degrees).
    • FluoVolt cells c2, c3, c4 and c6 are used, the four with exactly the nine-set escalation of Fig. 2A (n = 4); c5, whose escalation follows an extra 350 V set, is a variant.
    • All eight Di-8-ANEPPS escalation cells (c7 to c14) are used where the paper reports n = 5 without naming them; the criterion is also run on each of the 56 five-cell subsets.
    • The pixel size is taken as 0.267 um (16-um pixels of the iXon Ultra 897 behind the 60x objective, Methods 2.3); the files do not give it. Mean radii are then 7.8 to 15.1 um by cell (perimeter 7.84 to 15.06, equal-area 7.80 to 14.92; 7.84 to 12.85 over the cells used) against the paper's average of about 8 um; a variant uses the 0.192-um pixel that gives 8 um.
    • The charging time constant is 44.5 ns per um of radius, the fit of reference [20] Fig. 5C, as Methods 2.3 says.

    Under the top mapping the cathodic |dF| at the end of the pulse images is below the start in every set from 780 V/cm under every cell reading and both windows (experiment means, 10-image windows, 19.8 to 17.1, 22.2 to 19.7, 24.4 to 20.8 and 27.0 to 22.4%; p 0.034, 0.069, 0.037, 0.142).     • Factor, cell, trace, pixel and background choices as in fig3-dye-sensitivity.

  • ◐Partially reproducedFigure 5B; Results 3.5, paragraph 3

    By Eq. (2), the effective membrane conductivity barely affects charging while it stays far below cytosol and medium conductivities; a 10-fold rise reduces IMP by about 2% and a 100-fold rise by 12%.

    its 10-fold increase would go unnoticed, yielding just a ~2% IMP reduction, and a 100-fold increase would reduce IMP by just 12%.

    Figure 5B; Results 3.5, paragraph 3

    Eq. (2) with the constants of Methods 2.3 gives f = 1.498 for the intact membrane. A 10-fold rise reduces IMP by 1.33% under reading (a) and 1.20% under (b), against 2 +- 0.2; a 15.2-fold rise is needed for 2%. A 100-fold rise gives 11.87% and 11.75%, within 12 +- 1.2. The qualitative point holds, with 3.9% at 30-fold.

  • ?Not reproducedFigure 3 and legend; Results 3.3, paragraph 1verifier disagreed at firstrests on an assumptionuses data from outside the dataset

    Fitted linearly through the origin over -150 to +150 mV IMPC, FluoVolt changes by 26% and Di-8-ANEPPS by 9.1% dF per 100 mV, with correlation coefficients 0.96 and 0.78.

    The correlation coefficients of the fits equaled 0.96 for FluoVolt and 0.78 for Di-8 ANEPPS (due to a lower signal-to-noise ratio), with respective slope coefficients of 26% and 9.1% ΔF per 100 mV of IMP.

    Figure 3 and legend; Results 3.3, paragraph 1

    Under the adopted reading (top mapping, background subtracted, equal-area radius, all responding cells, the four sets from 140 to 336 V/cm) FluoVolt gives 6.10% dF per 100 mV (26 +- 2.6) with r 0.775 (Pearson) and 0.743 (uncentred), against 0.96 +- 0.096, and Di-8-ANEPPS 2.37% (9.1 +- 0.91) with r 0.481 and 0.402 (0.78 +- 0.078). Over the 16 top-mapping readings the slopes are 2.72 to 6.10 and 0.83 to 2.37; by experiment 5.36 to 6.64 and 2.20 to 2.53. The slope falls as the factor rises, but not as one over the factor, because the sets at or below 360 V/cm change with it. On a grid from 1.40 to 2.30 V/cm per V in steps of 0.02 the FluoVolt slope is within tolerance at 1.40 to 1.76 and the Di-8-ANEPPS slope at 1.82 to 2.16, with r within tolerance for both dyes throughout (0.90 to 0.92 and 0.79 to 0.84); no factor on that grid or in the sweep from 1.5 to 8 meets the criterion for both dyes, so the result is not reproduced at the factors run. The factors matching each slope, about 1.4 to 2.2, are near the 1.5 to 1.6 implied by the low-field response and below the 4.64 to 7.19 of the electrode geometry. Under the bottom variant, which was set from this same response, the slopes are 23.98 and 10.52 with r 0.908 and 0.824. None of 32 readings meets the criterion for both dyes. With the edge trace the slopes are 4.5 and 1.8 at the adopted factor.

    How the test differed from the paper’s method

    • The pulse is given in volts; the factor to V/cm is assumed to be 5.6 V/cm per V (1400 V/cm at 250 V, the top mapping). A two-dimensional model of the electrodes of Methods 2.2 (rods 0.5 mm in diameter, 1.6 mm apart) gives 4.64 to 7.19 V/cm per V at the midpoint, depending on whether 1.6 mm is the gap or the centre distance. The bottom mapping (43 V/cm at 25 V, 1.72 V/cm per V) matches the paper's dye sensitivities, which fig3-dye-sensitivity tests, and is run as a variant. Every criterion is also run over factors from 1.5 to 8 V/cm per V.
    • Each field of view holds 3 to 6 cells; the paper used one cell per experiment and does not say which. Experiment means (adopted), the cell nearest the image centre and all cells are run.
    • The membrane trace is found automatically (peak of the baseline image along rays from the cell centre, 6 samples 1 pixel apart), not drawn by hand; 360 points at equal angles stand for the resampling along the perimeter (Methods 2.3). In some cells the trace is drawn inwards by bright spots inside the cell, and one object in c7 is two touching cells; a trace that follows the cell edge is run as a variant over every criterion and changes no result at the adopted factor.
    • Pixel size 0.267 um (16-um camera pixels over the 60x objective of Methods 2.3) is assumed; the files give none. The mean radius is 6.7 um with the peak trace and 9.9 um with the edge trace, against the about 8 um the paper uses in Eq. (2).
    • Frame 0 and other dark frames (laser misfires) are left out, so most baselines have 39 images.
    • Background subtraction (median of cell-free pixels of the same image) is adopted; the paper states it only for Ca2+ imaging (Methods 2.4). No subtraction and the camera offset alone are run as variants.

    What the paper reports

    Under the top mapping, normalized to the 150 V/cm set (25 V), no reading meets the criterion. At 25 V both poles share a bleaching offset (mean of the poles -3.9% in Di-8-ANEPPS), which makes the anodic |dF| at 150 V/cm too large and the cathodic one too small. The anodic pole with experiment means and per-cell normalization (the measured values) gives 1.46 at 560 V/cm (1.5 +- 0.15) and 1.62 at 760 V/cm (2 +- 0.2), and 1.72, 1.76 and 2.08 at 1000 to 1400 V/cm, so it exceeds 2 only at 1400; every datapoint of that reading is below the line at p < 0.01. Its values are nearest the paper's, but it is the reading the offset affects most. The cathodic ratios are 0.63 to 0.94. The mean of the poles, in which the offset cancels, gives 0.55 to 0.85 with experiment means, so the measured polarization lies above the linear line. With per-cell normalization over all cells the mean of the poles gives ratios such as -10.7 and 16.9, because the 150 V/cm values are near zero; those ratios carry no information. Only factors 5.6 and 6.25 have every level, and neither meets the criterion.

    How the test differed from the paper’s method

    • The 560 and 760 V/cm of Results 3.7 are the 570 and 780 V/cm sets (claims file header).

  • ✓ReproducedResults, 3.6 Electroporative uptake of Ca2+, paragraph 2; Figure 6A

    A single 1-us pulse causes clear Ca2+ entry into CHO cells at 1000 and 1200 V/cm, among field strengths escalated from 0 to 1200 V/cm.

    Exposure to a single 1-μs pulse at escalating field strengths, from 0 to 1200 V/cm established a clear peak of Ca2+ entry at 1000 and 1200 V/cm

    Results, 3.6 Electroporative uptake of Ca2+, paragraph 2; Figure 6A

    Under M1 (1000 and 1200 V/cm at 250 and 300 V) three of the four readings hold at both fields. The end value (last 10 frames) is above the 0 V/cm group by 6.58 and 8.19% (Student's t-test p = 4.9e-09 and 3.0e-11), and the peak is above zero (2.30 and 5.35%, p = 3.3e-07 and 5.0e-07) and above the 0 V/cm peak (p = 6.1e-06 and 1.9e-08); the group means at both fields exceed those at 650 V/cm or less (0 and 150 V, at most -10.26% for the end value and 0.55% for the peak). The end value against zero fails, as it is negative at both fields (-5.44 and -3.83%), with the sham at -12.02%. Under M2 (300 and 350 V) the same three readings hold. With fields of view as the unit, the peak less the same field of view's sham is 2.42% at 250 V (4 fields of view, p = 0.0004) and 5.02% at 300 V (5, p = 0.0002). The end and peak readings compare later recordings with the sham, which is always recorded first, while the decline of the signal is steepest in the first recording (pre-pulse slope -3.97% per min in the first recording, -0.41 to -1.46% per min in the fifth to seventh). The step of dF at the pulse (5 frames after less 5 before), which this does not affect, is above the sham's at 250 V (0.35 +- 0.17%, p = 0.025) and 300 V (1.78 +- 0.31%, p = 3.2e-07), but not at 150 or 200 V (p = 0.13 and 0.10); at 250 V it is not above zero on its own (p = 0.058). So the entry at 1000 V/cm under M1 is small. The single-pulse session holds 27 cells in 6 fields of view, and three sets of fields of view give 14 cells, so the 14 cells of Fig. 6A cannot be identified. The results hold with regions of 0.5 and 1.0 of the radius and end windows of 5 and 20 frames.

    How the test differed from the paper’s method

    • The files give generator voltages and the paper field strengths, with no conversion in either. Two readings are run. M1, 3.9 V/cm per V, is adopted as the main reading (an assumption, as the paper gives no conversion), because the single-pulse voltages then fall on the paper's field series 570 to 1400 V/cm and the six train voltages nearest the fields labelled in Fig. 6B (230, 570, 650, 780, 900, 1000 V/cm) have group sizes close to the n the figure gives (28, 38, 36, 68, 47, 39 against 28, 37, 34, 54, 35, 36). M2, 3.3 V/cm per V, puts the train voltages 70, 200 and 230 V on the 230, 650 and 780 V/cm named in Section 3.6 and is shown as a variant. A named field is assigned to the nearest voltage. Neither factor follows from the electrode gap (V/d is 10 V/cm per V for the 1.00 mm of the files, 6.25 for the 1.6 mm of Methods 2.2). The outcome is the same under both.
    • Cells are found by an Otsu threshold and watershed of the pre-pulse mean image, with a circular region of 0.7 of each cell's equivalent radius and the median of pixels more than 15 px from any cell as background; the paper drew the regions by hand (Methods 2.4) and these are standard choices, shown with regions of 0.5 and 1.0 of the radius, end windows of 5 and 20 frames, and a background taken as the 10th percentile of the cell-free pixels.
    • Every detected cell in every file is used, as the dataset does not say which cells the paper used.
    • The paper names no test for this panel; the claims file's readings (one-sample t-test or Student's t-test against 0 V/cm, end value or peak) are all run.
  • ✓ReproducedResults, 3.6 Electroporative uptake of Ca2+, paragraph 2; Figure 6Aquestion to the reader

    After a single 1-us pulse at 780 V/cm the Ca2+ signal rises only subtly, at the detection limit, and not significantly.

    A subtle ΔF increase after exposure at 780 V/cm was at the detection limit and not statistically significant

    Results, 3.6 Electroporative uptake of Ca2+, paragraph 2; Figure 6A

    The criterion's test, a one-sample t-test of the end value against zero, finds no increase at 780 V/cm, with -7.42 +- 0.52% under M1 (200 V, n = 24) and -5.44 +- 0.55% under M2 (250 V, n = 18), one-sided p = 1 for both. That test cannot detect an increase here, as it finds none at 1000 and 1200 V/cm either (-5.44 and -3.83% under M1), so the outcome carries little information. Against the 0 V/cm group the end value is higher by 4.60% (M1, p = 1.3e-06), and the peak is 1.76 +- 0.61% (one-sample p = 0.0086; against the sham peak p = 0.021); both comparisons include the slowing of the decline with recording order. The step of dF at the pulse against the 0 V/cm group, which detects 1000 and 1200 V/cm under M1 (p = 0.025 and 3.2e-07), finds no increase at 780 V/cm under M1 (0.15 +- 0.16%, p = 0.10), and finds one under M2, where 780 V/cm is 250 V (p = 0.025). The step is the only reading not confounded by recording order, but it separates 1000 V/cm from the sham only barely (p = 0.025, and p = 0.058 against zero), and the steps at 780 and 1000 V/cm under M1 do not differ (0.15 +- 0.16 and 0.35 +- 0.17%, p = 0.40), so it is weak evidence for the absence at 780 V/cm. Whether the criterion's test can stand for the paper's is asked of a reader (questions/001521.yaml).

    How the test differed from the paper’s method

    • The files give generator voltages and the paper field strengths, with no conversion in either. Two readings are run. M1, 3.9 V/cm per V, is adopted as the main reading (an assumption, as the paper gives no conversion), because the single-pulse voltages then fall on the paper's field series 570 to 1400 V/cm and the six train voltages nearest the fields labelled in Fig. 6B (230, 570, 650, 780, 900, 1000 V/cm) have group sizes close to the n the figure gives (28, 38, 36, 68, 47, 39 against 28, 37, 34, 54, 35, 36). M2, 3.3 V/cm per V, puts the train voltages 70, 200 and 230 V on the 230, 650 and 780 V/cm named in Section 3.6 and is shown as a variant. A named field is assigned to the nearest voltage. Neither factor follows from the electrode gap (V/d is 10 V/cm per V for the 1.00 mm of the files, 6.25 for the 1.6 mm of Methods 2.2). The outcome is the same under both.
    • Cells are found by an Otsu threshold and watershed of the pre-pulse mean image, with a circular region of 0.7 of each cell's equivalent radius and the median of pixels more than 15 px from any cell as background; the paper drew the regions by hand (Methods 2.4) and these are standard choices, shown with regions of 0.5 and 1.0 of the radius, end windows of 5 and 20 frames, and a background taken as the 10th percentile of the cell-free pixels.
    • Every detected cell in every file is used, as the dataset does not say which cells the paper used.

    The mean dF over the last 10 frames is below zero at 1000 and 1200 V/cm under M1 (-5.44 +- 0.55% and -3.83 +- 0.71%) and at 1000 V/cm under M2 (-3.83%); at 1200 V/cm under M2 (350 V) it is +1.24 +- 1.95%, p = 0.53.     • Cells are found by an Otsu threshold and watershed of the pre-pulse mean image, with a circular region of 0.7 of each cell's equivalent radius and the median of pixels more than 15 px from any cell as background; the paper drew the regions by hand (Methods 2.4) and these are standard choices, shown with regions of 0.5 and 1.0 of the radius, end windows of 5 and 20 frames, and a background taken as the 10th percentile of the cell-free pixels.
    • Every detected cell in every file is used, as the dataset does not say which cells the paper used.
  • ?Not reproducedResults, 3.6 Electroporative uptake of Ca2+, paragraph 3; Figure 6B

    With trains of sixty 1-us pulses at 6 Hz (each cell tested once, 224 cells), a strong Ca2+ response occurs in 28% of cells at 780 V/cm and in nearly all cells at higher fields.

    A strong Ca2+ response was recorded in 28% of cells exposed at 780 V/cm and in nearly all cells at higher intensities

    Results, 3.6 Electroporative uptake of Ca2+, paragraph 3; Figure 6B

    No reading meets the criterion. The fields above 780 V/cm are taken as those of Fig. 6B, 900 and 1000 V/cm (230 and 250 V under M1, 250 and 300 V under M2). The fraction of strong responders at 780 V/cm is 2.9 to 22.1% under M1 (200 V, 68 cell exposures) and 44.7 to 78.7% under M2 (230 V, 47), against 28 +- 2.8%, over six rules, including "above 3 s.d." read literally as a threshold of 3 s.d. of the reference group. The lower of the two fractions at the higher fields is 44.7 to 78.7% under M1 and 61.5 to 79.5% under M2, against at least 90%. With regions of 0.5 and 1.0 of the radius the criterion is met in none of the 36 combinations of region, conversion and rule. The six voltage groups that correspond to the Fig. 6B fields hold 256 cell exposures under M1 (224 under M2), against 224; in all, the dataset holds 311 exposures to trains of 82 distinct cells in 9 fields of view, each recorded at 1 to 7 voltages, where the paper states 224 cells each tested once (measured gives the 780 V/cm fraction under M1 and rule (a) against 0 V/cm, and the cells in the M1 groups of the Fig. 6B fields). The figure's group sizes are close to the dataset's exposures, so the paper may have counted exposures as cells. Taking only the first train of each field of view, 9.1 to 18.2% respond at 200 V and 90.9 to 100% at 250 V (11 cells each). The end values carry the decline of the sham signal (-15.7% in the train session) that the Fig. 6B traces do not show. These numbers use the criterion's end window of 10 frames; with 5 frames and a region of 0.5 of the radius the 780 V/cm fraction under M1 and the rule above the largest of the lowest field is 27.9%, within tolerance, but the higher fields reach 76.6 and 82.1%, not 90%. With a background taken as the 10th percentile of the cell-free pixels, which dim unsegmented cells affect less (the background falls from 1.81 to 1.21 times the corrected cell signal), the criterion is met in none of 12 combinations of conversion and rule (780 V/cm 2.9 to 23.5% under M1, 42.6 to 91.5% under M2; lower higher-field fraction at most 87.2%).

    How the test differed from the paper’s method

    • The files give generator voltages and the paper field strengths, with no conversion in either. Two readings are run. M1, 3.9 V/cm per V, is adopted as the main reading (an assumption, as the paper gives no conversion), because the single-pulse voltages then fall on the paper's field series 570 to 1400 V/cm and the six train voltages nearest the fields labelled in Fig. 6B (230, 570, 650, 780, 900, 1000 V/cm) have group sizes close to the n the figure gives (28, 38, 36, 68, 47, 39 against 28, 37, 34, 54, 35, 36). M2, 3.3 V/cm per V, puts the train voltages 70, 200 and 230 V on the 230, 650 and 780 V/cm named in Section 3.6 and is shown as a variant. A named field is assigned to the nearest voltage. Neither factor follows from the electrode gap (V/d is 10 V/cm per V for the 1.00 mm of the files, 6.25 for the 1.6 mm of Methods 2.2). The outcome is the same under both.
    • Cells are found by an Otsu threshold and watershed of the pre-pulse mean image, with a circular region of 0.7 of each cell's equivalent radius and the median of pixels more than 15 px from any cell as background; the paper drew the regions by hand (Methods 2.4) and these are standard choices, shown with regions of 0.5 and 1.0 of the radius, end windows of 5 and 20 frames, and a background taken as the 10th percentile of the cell-free pixels.
    • The cells were tested more than once in the dataset; all exposures are used, and the first exposure of each field of view is shown as a variant.
    • Reading (b), above 3 s.d. of the reference group, is run as above its mean plus 3 s.d. and, literally, above 3 s.d.

    What the paper reports

    • 650 V/cm end-of-recording signal against zero: one sample t-test, p < 0.001
    • 650 V/cm against 230 V/cm: single-tailed t-test, p < 0.05

    The statement's tests fail under both conversions. Under M1 the mean end value at 170 V (650 V/cm) is -9.17 +- 0.83%, significantly below zero (two-sided p = 6.4e-13, one-sided p = 1 for above zero), and not above the 70 V group (single-tailed p = 0.20). Under M2 the 200 V mean is -1.98 +- 1.77% (p = 0.27 against zero) and above the 70 V group (single-tailed p = 0.0023). The count is met under one combination of 36 (M1, 170 V, rule above the largest of the lowest field, region 0.7 of the radius, 2 of 36, 5.6%); measured gives the first reading, rule (a) against 0 V/cm under M1, 0 cells. Regions of 0.5 and 1.0 give 1 cell (2.8%) under the same rule, the other five rules give 0 under M1, and M2 (200 V, 68 exposures) gives 2 to 17 cells (2.9 to 25.0%). As the tests fail under both conversions and every rule, the outcome does not rest on the choice. The end values carry the decline of the sham signal. With a background taken as the 10th percentile of the cell-free pixels the 650 V/cm mean is -7.37% under M1 and -1.54% under M2 (one-sided p = 1.0 and 0.87 for above zero), and the count of 2 cells (5.9%) is met in none of 12 combinations. In round 1 this claim was blocked as methods_underspecified; the statement's tests are fully specified and fail, so it is not reproduced.

M2, 3.3 V/cm per V, puts the train voltages 70, 200 and 230 V on the 230, 650 and 780 V/cm named in Section 3.6 and is shown as a variant. A named field is assigned to the nearest voltage. Neither factor follows from the electrode gap (V/d is 10 V/cm per V for the 1.00 mm of the files, 6.25 for the 1.6 mm of Methods 2.2). The outcome is the same under both.
    • Cells are found by an Otsu threshold and watershed of the pre-pulse mean image, with a circular region of 0.7 of each cell's equivalent radius and the median of pixels more than 15 px from any cell as background; the paper drew the regions by hand (Methods 2.4) and these are standard choices, shown with regions of 0.5 and 1.0 of the radius, end windows of 5 and 20 frames, and a background taken as the 10th percentile of the cell-free pixels.

    The pulsed-laser potentiometric imaging (FluoVolt and Di-8-ANEPPS) is not in this dandiset, which holds only Fluo-4 time-lapses. The paper's Data availability says data are available on request. Six other dandisets of the same lab name this paper in their metadata (001515, 001516, 001517, 001518, 001525 and 001526) and by their titles hold the potentiometric experiments; they are targets of their own, and the claims file does not name them as data the paper relies on, so they were not used here.

  • ✓ReproducedResults, 3.7, paragraph 3; Figure 7Arests on an assumption

    After a conditioning pulse of up to 1000 V/cm, polarization by the 150 V/cm test pulse 5 us later still follows a cosine of the angle to the field, while 1200 and 1400 V/cm conditioning pulses markedly reduce charging at the cell poles.

    Polarization still followed a cosine function after conditioning PEFs of up to 1000 V/cm. However, 1200 and 1400 V/cm conditioning PEFs markedly hindered charging by the test pulse at cell poles, indicating a persistent membrane permeabilization.

    Results, 3.7, paragraph 3; Figure 7A

    After the first verification round the membrane trace was corrected. The overlays of the data check show that the first round's trace, the plain intensity peak along each ray, fell on bright dye inside cells c5 and c9 (a median 11.7 and 8.9 px inside the cell's outer edge); the trace used now is the intensity peak within 8 px inside the outer edge (2.5 to 4.8 px inside it in every cell). With it the outcome changes from partially reproduced to reproduced. Under the main reading (edge trace, six 5-us cells, annulus background, dark frames dropped), with the unpaired test of the amended criterion no pole reading differs from the 150/150 benchmark after 360 to 1000 V/cm conditioning (smallest p 0.059, mean of poles at 1000 V/cm), so the cosine part holds under reading (b). Pole dF is below the benchmark after 1200 and 1400 V/cm at the cathodic pole (unpaired p 0.022 and 0.0010) and for the mean of both poles (p 0.0013 and 0.0002); the anodic pole reaches p 0.086 at 1200 V/cm. Cosine amplitudes are 12.6% dF (benchmark), 10.4% (1000 V/cm) and 5.0% (1400 V/cm), R^2 0.73, 0.68 and -0.55 (A cos(theta - phi) without an offset term). Reading (a), the s.e. band, fails in every variant, and under the main reading at both poles at the control and at 1000 V/cm, because the profiles carry a uniform negative offset (-4.3% dF at the control); the baseline images fade by about 0.10% per frame in 43 of 44 files, which predicts -5.16% over all files against a measured -3.67% (r 0.18 over files), the sign of the bleaching the paper reports (Results 3.2) but not its size, so the offset's source is not established. With an offset term, a diagnostic outside the criterion, the fit lies within the s.e. at both poles up to 1000 V/cm and not at 1200 or 1400 V/cm. Over 22 variants the claim holds in 19; it fails in the 3 with the first round's peak trace, where the 1200 V/cm hindrance is not significant at any pole, so the 19 are not independent confirmations; the outcome turns on the edge trace. With the paired test, a variant, it holds in 21 of 22. The window of the edge trace (8 px) is a choice; with all six cells the claim holds under every window from 4 to 12 px (hindered at the cathodic pole and the mean of poles from 6 to 10 px, at every pole with 4 px, for the mean of poles only with 12 px). The trace of c5 dips into the cathodic pole arc in its 760 and 1000 V/cm files (8.8 and 8.2 px below its running median), strengths at which the cosine part holds; in c5's first file the notch lies 22 to 35 degrees from the cathodic pole. The outcome rests on the trace, the rank mapping of strengths and the single-pulse benchmark.

    How the test differed from the paper’s method

    • The perimeter trace is found automatically, where the paper draws "the 6-pixel-wide trace ... along the cell perimeter" by hand in MetaMorph (Methods 2.3). The membrane at each angle is the intensity peak within 8 px inside the cell's outer edge (the steepest outward fall of intensity), an assumption adopted after the first round's plain intensity peak was seen to fall inside two cells; that trace is shown as a variant, and so are windows of 4, 6, 10 and 12 px.
    • The analysed cell is the largest object in each subject's field (an assumption; the paper says one cell per experiment, Fig. 2 legend, and does not say how it was chosen).
    • The field direction in image coordinates is not given by the dataset; it is the A-weighted circular mean of cosine-fit phases over all 5-us files (-75.6 degrees, per-file sd 6.8), and from the control files alone as a variant (-73.9 degrees). 0 degrees is the depolarized pole, as Fig. 7A plots it.
    • Conditioning strengths are mapped by rank from the files' generator volts (100 to 350 V) to the Fig. 7A labels 360 to 1400 V/cm, and the single 50 V pulse, recorded first in every cell, is taken as the 150/150 benchmark; an assumption, as the files give no field strengths and no 150/150 pair. Read as steps of 100 V from 0.15 kV/cm at 50 V, the strengths in the files' general description would put the levels 0 to 11% lower, with the same order.
    • Six 5-us cells are in the dataset where the paper reports n = 5; all six are used, and each five-cell subset is shown.
    • Background and dark frames are open in the paper. Main reading adopted as an assumption, background removed (median of an annulus 1.6 to 2.2 radii) and frames without a laser flash dropped; the paper's own scale (Fig. 7A about 13% dF at 150/150; 9.1% dF per 100 mV in Results 3.3, about 16% dF at 150 V/cm) is met only with a background removed (12.6 to 12.9% against 5.6%), and the files' camera floor is about 209 counts under a membrane trace of 451 to 692. All four combinations are shown.
    • The criterion's pole dF is tested under the pole readings of fig7b-test-pulse-unchanged-to-760 (cathodic, anodic, mean of both), with its test, the unpaired two-sample t-test against the control group (the paired test shown as a variant); reading (a) compares the fit at each pole with the s.e. of the pole mean over cells, for the legend's gray outline.

After the first verification round the membrane trace was corrected (see fig7a-test-pulse-cosine-up-to-1000); the outcome stays reproduced. Under the main reading the unpaired t-test finds no difference from control at 360 to 1000 V/cm at either pole (smallest p 0.098, anodic at 760 V/cm; cathodic p 0.21 to 0.99). The paired test, a variant, finds no difference at either pole under the main reading (smallest p 0.098) and in 18 of 22 variants; the one-sample test in 21 of 22. For the mean of both poles, which the criterion does not test for absence, the paired test gives p 0.018 at 760 V/cm and 0.016 at 1000 V/cm (unpaired 0.14 and 0.059). The trace window changes the outcome; the absence holds with windows of 6 to 12 px and fails with 4 px (anodic pole at 760 V/cm, p 0.028), and without c5 it fails under every window (anodic 760 V/cm, p 0.010 to 0.046). The trace of c5 dips into the cathodic pole arc in its 760 and 1000 V/cm files, where its cathodic dF is -3.3% and -1.9%; these values pull the cathodic pole towards a difference, and none is found (unpaired p 0.74 and 0.21). With the first round's trace the cathodic pole was at 133% of control after 360 V/cm (paired p 0.0038); with the edge trace it is at 99.5%. The outcome rests on the trace and its window, the rank mapping of strengths and the single-pulse benchmark, assumptions shared with fig7a-test-pulse-cosine-up-to-1000.

    How the test differed from the paper’s method

    • Same trace, cell, field direction, strength mapping, benchmark, cell count and main reading as fig7a-test-pulse-cosine-up-to-1000.

  • ✓ReproducedResults, section 3.2, paragraph 4; Figure 2Crests on an assumption

    At 1000 V/cm, cathodic pole dF fell from 40 +- 10% at the start of the set to 26.6 +- 8% at its end, a trend that was not statistically significant.

    In contrast, at an electroporating field strength of 1000 V/cm, ΔF at the cathodic pole decreased from 40 ± 10% at the beginning of the set to 26.6 ± 8% at the end of it. Although not statistically significant (p > 0.05)

    Results, section 3.2, paragraph 4; Figure 2C

    What the paper reports

    • 2-tailed paired t-test (the test named in the Fig. 2 legend), p > 0.05 (n = 8 cells)

    Background recording subtracted, reading (b) (PEF images 1 to 10 and 51 to 60), 31.7 +- 4.4% to 21.0 +- 5.2%, n = 11, paired t-test p = 0.094, end lower in 8 of 11 cells; this is the reading that matches and is given as measured. Readings (a) 40.7 to 22.4% (p = 0.008) and (c) 35.4 to 23.4% (p = 0.006) have values within tolerance but p below 0.05. Annulus, reading (b), 33.5 to 21.5% with p = 0.035, so no window meets the criterion under the annulus; without subtraction (13.3 and 8.5%) and with the darkest-pixel floor (24.0 and 15.7%) the start is outside the tolerance. With F0 from images 2 to 40 the start under (b) is 28.5%, just outside. Among the 165 choices of 8 of the 11 cells the criterion is met in 91 (background recording), 100 (annulus) and none without subtraction or with the floor. With per-cell pole angles it is met under the background recording (p = 0.080), not under the annulus (p = 0.028). In round 1 (annulus, c4 traced on internal staining) the match was 31.9 to 21.0% with p = 0.051. The match rests on the background reading, and the background recording was adopted after the annulus reading had stopped meeting this claim, so it rests on a choice made after the results. If the darkest pixels are the camera offset (floor reading), the claim is not met.

    How the test differed from the paper’s method

    • Background subtraction is an assumption. Section 2.3 describes dF = 100 (F - F0)/F0 with no background step, section 2.4 subtracts the background fluorescence of a cell-free region for Fluo-4, and the claims file names both readings. The adopted reading subtracts, for 2025-05-01, the mean of the cell-free background recording made with each cell (the files call it a recording in the background region without cells, so the experimenters recorded it with every cell of that session), point by point along the trace; for 2025-05-06, which has no background recordings, the per-image median of a cell-free annulus 2 to 3 radii from the cell, whose level the background recordings of the first session match within 0.97 to 1.08 times. Cell-free background is 52 to 54% of the trace intensity, and 65 to 66 counts above the darkest pixels (no dark frame or camera offset is given), and the paper's own values at 1000 V/cm (40 and 26.6%) are on the scale of the subtracted readings and about 3 times the unsubtracted ones (40/13.3 = 3.0 and 26.6/8.5 = 3.1 under window (b)). The notebook shows every reading (background recording, annulus, darkest-pixel floor, none). Round 1 adopted the annulus for both sessions; the background recording was adopted in the revision after round 1, after the results under both readings had been seen, and in that revision the annulus reading stopped meeting fig2c-1000-within-set while the background recording met it. The choice is to be read as made after the results.
    • The analysed cell is the one nearest the image centre of the cell's sham recording (an assumption; the files do not say which of the one to eight cells found in each field was analysed, and section 2.2 centres the field of view between the electrodes). The notebook draws every traced membrane on its baseline image.
    • The 6-pixel-wide perimeter trace is found automatically as the outer ridge of the membrane staining in the sham recording's baseline image and moved by the measured drift (at most 2 pixels), where the paper draws it by hand in MetaMorph (section 2.3); the 360 points are spaced by 1 degree of angle, not by equal arc length. For 2025-05-01 c4 the default rule (steepest outward fall) put the trace on internal staining (median radius 21.2 px), so that cell is traced with the outermost strong fall (34.1 px); the other cells keep the default rule, which the overlays show on the membrane (c3's trace cuts inside the cell on one side, away from the poles).
    • The cathodic pole angle is the median over the session's cells of the phase of a cosine fitted to the polarization profile at non-electroporating fields (83.0 degrees for 1 us, 82.1 degrees for 0.3 us, close to straight up, as in Fig. 1); the paper places the poles by the field direction. Per-cell phases (73.1 to 93.6 degrees) are shown as a variant.
    • Field strengths are the paper's labels (Fig. 2C; the Fig. 4D axis labels 357, 760, 1000, 1200, 1420, 1630, 2050, 2473 and 3320 V/cm, read from the figure on page 6 of the PDF) matched by order to the pulse amplitudes in volts that the files give; the labels are not proportional to the volts.
    • 11 cells are used where the paper reports n = 8; the dataset does not say which 8. Four cells depart from the described order of sets (c3 and c4 before the 1000 V/cm exposure, c1 and c11 after); sets are matched by their rank at each field. The notebook applies the criteria without c4, without c3 and c4, without all four, and to every choice of 8 of the 11 cells.

  • ⊘BlockedResults, section 3.2, paragraph 2; Figure 2A· Data missing from the dataset

    In FluoVolt-loaded cells, pole dF rose with field strength until apparent saturation at 66% (255 mV) at 360 V/cm at the cathodic pole and -83% (-320 mV) at 570 V/cm at the anodic pole; stronger fields did not increase the anodic response and decreased the cathodic one.

    ΔF increased proportionally to the electric field strength until reaching apparent saturation at 66% ΔF (255 mV IMP) at 360 V/cm at the cathodic pole, and −83% ΔF (−320 mV IMP) at 570 V/cm at the anodic pole. Further escalation of the electric field strength did not increase the dye response at the anodic pole and even decreased it at the cathodic pole.

Notes on the paper

What the attempts found about the paper itself: a method or rule it does not give, a place where it disagrees with itself, published code that does something else. What they found about the data is on each dataset’s page.

  • Paper inconsistentEq. (2) with the constants of Methods 2.3 gives a 1.33% IMP reduction for a 10-fold rise of membrane conductivity, where Results 3.5 states about 2%; the 100-fold value (11.87%) matches the stated 12%.
  • Paper inconsistentResults 3.5 states that a 10-fold rise in effective membrane conductivity yields a ~2% IMP reduction by Eq. (2), but Eq. (2) with the constants of Methods 2.3 gives 1.33% (1.20% relative to the intact membrane); a 2% reduction needs a 15- to 16-fold rise. The 100-fold value (12%) agrees.
  • Methods not availableQuestion for the authors. Where were the background ROIs of the strobe images placed, and how far from the membrane? Reference [20] says near the poles outside the cell. Here a background 2 to 4 um out, 8 to 10 um out, or none changes the pole dF by up to a factor of three (FluoVolt cathodic at 360 V/cm 78.5, 49.4 and 23.7%), and the 8 to 10 um reading was assumed. A sweep of 6-pixel bands from 8 to 44 pixels out shows the band 14 to 20 pixels (4 to 5 um) out would bring the FluoVolt saturation values, both Fig. 3 slopes and the first-10 Di-8-ANEPPS maximum within the paper's values.
  • Methods not availableQuestion for the authors. What is the pixel size of the images, and how was the cell radius measured? With 16-um camera pixels behind the 60x objective (0.267 um) the cells have mean radii of 7.8 to 15.1 um, larger than the 8 um the paper gives as typical, and the dye slopes of Fig. 3 come out 21.6 and 7.4% per 100 mV; with 0.192 um (radius 8 um) they come out 26.6 and 9.5, as in the paper.
  • Methods not availableQuestion for the authors. Which generator amplitude in the files corresponds to each field strength of the paper (is 25 V the 43 V/cm set, and so on), and which cells form the n = 4 FluoVolt and n = 5 Di-8-ANEPPS groups of Fig. 2 and 4? Both were assumed here (amplitudes matched in order; four FluoVolt cells with exactly nine sets; all eight Di-8-ANEPPS cells, with the 56 five-cell subsets shown).
  • Paper inconsistentResults 3.5 says a 10-fold rise of the effective membrane conductivity reduces IMP by about 2%; Eq. (2) with the constants of Methods 2.3 gives 1.3% (a 2% reduction needs a 15-fold rise). The 100-fold value (12%) agrees with the equation.
  • Methods not availableQuestions for the paper's authors. (1) What field strength in V/cm does each pulse amplitude in the files correspond to, and are these the escalations of Fig. 2A, 2B and 3 or other experiments? The attempt assumed 1400 V/cm at 250 V (5.6 V/cm per volt), from the electrode geometry, and ran 1.5 to 8. (2) Which cell in each field of view was analysed? The attempt ran experiment means, the cell nearest the image centre and all cells. (3) Was background subtracted from the laser images, as for Ca2+ (Methods 2.4)? The attempt subtracted the cell-free median; without it dF halves. (4) What is the pixel size? The attempt assumed 0.267 um.
  • Paper inconsistentEq. (2) with the constants the Methods give (lambda_i 0.4 S/m, lambda_o 1.64 S/m, R 8 um, d 5 nm, lambda_m 3e-7 S/m) gives a 1.3% IMP reduction for a 10-fold rise in membrane conductivity, while Results 3.5 says about 2%; the 100-fold value (11.9%) agrees with the stated 12%.
  • Methods not availableQuestion for the authors. How were generator voltages converted to the field strengths of Fig. 6, and was the same conversion used for single pulses and trains? 3.9 V/cm per V fits the fields and group sizes of Fig. 6B and was adopted; 3.3 V/cm per V puts the train voltages on the three fields named in the text and was run as a variant. No outcome changes between the two.
  • Methods not availableQuestion for the authors. How was a strong Ca2+ response defined in Fig. 6B, how large was the region over each cell, and which cells formed each group? The count of 2 responders at 650 V/cm is met under one of 36 combinations of conversion, rule and region size here.
  • Methods not availableQuestion for the authors. Unpulsed cells lose 12% (single-pulse session) and 16% (train session) of their background-corrected Fluo-4 signal by the end of a recording, relative to the pre-pulse mean; the raw cell signal falls by 8.7 and 7.5% while the cell-free background falls by 1.0 and 0.6%. The 0 V/cm trace of Fig. 6A rises by about 1% and the Fig. 6B traces are flat. Were the dF traces of Fig. 6 corrected for this decline, or do they come from other recordings, and which test gave "not statistically significant" at 780 V/cm? Without a correction the end-of-recording dF at 1000 and 1200 V/cm is below zero, and fig6a-ca-sustained is not reproduced.
  • Paper inconsistentEq. 2 with the constants of Methods 2.3 gives a 1.3% reduction of pole IMP for a 10-fold increase of membrane conductivity, where Section 3.5 states about 2%.
  • OtherEach field of view was recorded first without a pulse and then at escalating voltages, and the decline of the Fluo-4 signal is steepest in the first recording (before the pulse, -3.97% per min in the first recording of the single-pulse session and -0.41 to -1.46% per min in the fifth to seventh, slowing with each recording; -11.4% per min in the first recording of the train session and -2.0 to -5.4% per min in later ones, without a steady trend). Field strength and recording order therefore go together, and comparisons with the sham, always recorded first, include part of this difference.
  • Selection not documentedSix cells (c5 to c10) have the paper's 5-us interval where Fig. 7 reports n = 5, and nothing says which five were used. Four more cells (c1 to c4) have a 1-us interval, which the paper does not report.
  • Methods not availableFor the paper's authors, whether a background was subtracted from the potentiometric images and whether images without a laser flash were kept when the 40 baseline and 60 pulse images were collapsed. Background removal doubles the dF scale here (5.6% to 12.6% at 150/150), and only the background-subtracted scale matches the paper's.
  • OtherThe images fade through each set of 100. Over the 40 baseline images the membrane trace intensity (background removed) falls by about 0.10% per image in 43 of 44 files of the 5-us cells, which would predict a uniform dF of -5.16% over the 60 pulse images; the profiles' mean dF is -3.67% over these files and -4.3% at the control, and the two correlate weakly over files (r 0.18). The sign is that of the Di-8-ANEPPS bleaching the paper reports (Results 3.2), the size does not match, and the offset's source is not established; nothing in the files corrects or marks it.
  • Methods not availableQuestion for the paper's authors. Was a background subtracted from the potentiometric images before computing dF, and if so which: the cell-free background recording made with each 2025-05-01 cell, a cell-free region of the same image, or the camera offset? Section 2.3 describes none; section 2.4 subtracts a cell-free region for Fluo-4. Cell-free background is 52 to 54% of the membrane intensity in these files, so dF without subtraction is about 40% of dF with it. This attempt assumed subtraction of the background recording (and of a cell-free annulus for 2025-05-06). The outcomes of fig2cd-nonelectroporating-stable (not reproduced with subtraction, reproduced without) and fig2c-1000-within-set (reproduced with the background recording only) depend on the answer.
  • Methods not availableQuestion for the paper's authors. Which cell in each field of view, and which 8 of the 11 cells of the 2025-05-01 session, form Fig. 2C and D, and are 10 cells of Fig. 4D in the dandiset (it holds 8)? This attempt used the cell nearest the image centre and every cell in the dandiset. Without c3 and c4, the two cells whose pre-exposure sets depart from the described order, all three Fig. 2C and D claims are met; among the 165 choices of 8 cells they are met together in 1 (background recording reading).
  • Paper inconsistentEq. (2) as printed, with the parameters of section 2.3, gives a 1.3% IMP reduction for a 10-fold increase of membrane conductivity, where section 3.5 states ~2% (the 100-fold value, 11.9%, matches the stated 12%).

Questions

Where the author or a verifier believed the claims file misread the paper, they asked, citing the paper, and a reader who had not seen the data answered from the paper alone, amending the claims file where it was wrong. The author then ran the claim as answered. Attempts before protocol pilot-11 also asked here for method details the claims file did not give.

  • openasked 2026-11-02about fig7a-cosine-profiles

    The claims file's header reads the dandiset's title and description as pointing at the two-pulse recovery experiment of Fig. 7 (Results 3.7), adds fig7a-cosine-profiles because "Fig. 7 is this dandiset's experiment", and plans collapsing 20 and 40 images as the only deviation. The files name FluoVolt with a FITC cube in all 72 sets, 1.00-mm electrodes, single pulses and pairs at 50 to 250 V (described as 0.15 to 1.57 kV/cm) and intervals of 1, 5, 25, 125 and 250 us, where Fig. 7 used Di-8-ANEPPS, 1.6-mm electrodes, conditioning at 150 to 1400 V/cm and a 5-us interval only. Should the header and the Fig. 7 claims (fig7a-cosine-profiles, fig7b-charging-during-pulse, fig7ab-test-pulse-recovery, fig7c-fast-conductivity-drop, fig7c-full-reseal-mild) say that the dandiset holds a FluoVolt two-pulse experiment the paper does not report, and that no claim can be tested on it?

    Passage. Each conditioning pulse was followed in 5 us with a 1-us, 150 V/cm test pulse (Results 3.7); Cells loaded with Di-8-ANEPPS (Fig. 7 legend); two tungsten rods 1.6 mm apart (Methods 2.2); and Future studies should focus on the time course of membrane recovery, perhaps using the same two-pulse protocol as in Fig. 7, but varying the time interval between the pulses (Discussion).

    Why it matters. The outcomes do not change. All five Fig. 7 claims are blocked as data_missing because the dye, field levels, electrode spacing and number of cells differ from the paper's, as the claims file's criteria name Di-8-ANEPPS and n = 5. If the reader kept the premise, a later attempt might be asked to test the Fig. 7 criteria on the dataset's FluoVolt 5-us pairs (c2, c9), which the notebook shows only as a variant.

    No reader has answered it yet.
  • openasked 2026-11-02about fig2a-fluovolt-stable

    Should the criterion of fig2a-fluovolt-stable allow for the number of tests it makes? Each start/end reading runs 24 paired t-tests at p < 0.05 with n = 4 (6 pulse comparisons at 1000 to 1400 V/cm and 18 baseline comparisons), and the claim fails if any one of them is significant. With no change at all, the chance that at least one of 24 independent tests falls below 0.05 is about 70% (1 - 0.95^24), so the criterion fails a stable recording more often than not. A reading that corrects for the number of tests (for example Bonferroni or Holm over the 24), or that asks for a change larger than some size in % dF, would test what the paper says.

    Passage. Without PEF (first 40 datapoints, or ~ 7 s in each set), measurements were stable, indicating no significant photosensitization or dye bleaching ... but the dye response in each set stayed stable through the 60 pulses applied (Results 3.2, paragraph 2); the Fig. 2 legend names a 2-tailed paired t-test. The paper reports no test for this statement and does not say how many comparisons it made.

    Why it matters. Under the adopted far background the claim fails on 2 significant tests of 24 under reading (a) and 5 under reading (b), with changes of 2.5 to 7.0% dF against responses of 40 to 70%. Under reading (b) the smallest p is below 0.0005 (360 V/cm cathodic baseline, -4.2%) and the next is 0.002, so a Bonferroni correction over 24 tests (p < 0.0021) would leave one or two significant baseline changes; under reading (a) it would leave none (smallest p 0.005), so the readings would disagree and the claim would become blocked (methods_underspecified) under the criterion's own rule for disagreeing readings.

    No reader has answered it yet.
  • openasked 2026-11-03about fig6a-780-not-significant

    The criterion judges the claim of absence by a one-sample t-test of each cell's end-of-recording dF against zero. Can that test stand for the paper's "not statistically significant", when the same claims file treats 1000 and 1200 V/cm as a clear entry? In this dataset that test finds no increase at 1000 and 1200 V/cm either, so it cannot detect the effect the claim says is absent at 780 V/cm. Should the test be the one that detects the entry at 1000 and 1200 V/cm (for example against the 0 V/cm group, or on the change at the pulse), with the claim of absence judged against that?

    Passage. Exposure to a single 1-us pulse at escalating field strengths, from 0 to 1200 V/cm established a clear peak of Ca2+ entry at 1000 and 1200 V/cm, and Ca2+ signal remained elevated through the 5-min observation period (Fig. 6A). A subtle dF increase after exposure at 780 V/cm was at the detection limit and not statistically significant. (Section 3.6, paragraph 2; Methods 2.5 name Student's t-test and the one-sample t-test.)

    Why it matters. Unpulsed cells decline to -12% by the end of the recording here, so the end value is below zero at 780, 1000 and 1200 V/cm (under 3.9 V/cm per V, -7.42, -5.44 and -3.83%) and the criterion's test gives "reproduced" without being able to find any increase. Against the 0 V/cm group the end value and the peak are significantly higher at 780 V/cm (p = 1.3e-06 and 0.021), which would give "not reproduced"; the step of dF at the pulse against the 0 V/cm group detects 1000 and 1200 V/cm (p = 0.025 and 3e-07) and not 780 V/cm (p = 0.10), which would give "reproduced". The verifier of round 1 reported the criterion's test as unable to detect an increase. The verifier of round 2 noted that the step reading is marginal at 1000 V/cm (p = 0.058 against zero) and that the steps at 780 and 1000 V/cm were not shown to differ; they do not (p = 0.40), so no reading here both detects 1000 V/cm clearly and finds 780 V/cm absent.

    No reader has answered it yet.
  • answered 2026-11-02asked 2026-11-02about fig7b-test-pulse-unchanged-to-760

    The criterion makes the claim methods_underspecified when the paired and the unpaired two-sided t-test disagree. Does the paper not settle the reading? Results 3.7 reports the 360 V/cm cathodic test-pulse point, which reaches about 120% of control, as p > 0.1, and the Fig. 7B legend tests the difference "from the undamaged membrane (gray area between horizontal dashed lines)", a band with its own spread. Both fit an unpaired comparison with the control group more than a paired one. Should the criterion take the unpaired test as the paper's, with the paired test as a variant?

    Passage. Also, an accidental ΔF peaking to 120% (p > 0.1, lower panel in Fig. 7B) was considered as 100% for interpolation. (Results 3.7, paragraph 6); * p < 0.05, ** p < 0.01 with a two-way t-test for the difference from the undamaged membrane (gray area between horizontal dashed lines). (Fig. 7B legend)

    Why it matters. On the dataset the cathodic test response after 360 V/cm is 133% of control; the paired test gives p = 0.0038 and the unpaired p = 0.61. The paired test finds a difference in 16 of 18 variants and the unpaired test in none, so the claim is blocked under the criterion as written and would be reproduced (80.0% of control at 1000 V/cm, within 80 +- 8) under the unpaired reading. The same choice bears on fig7a-test-pulse-cosine-up-to-1000 and fig7b-test-pulse-reduced-1200-1400, which take their tests from this claim.

    The reader’s answer. Changed. The paper settles the reading. The Fig. 7B legend tests "the difference from the undamaged membrane (gray area between horizontal dashed lines)", a comparison with the control group and its spread; the Methods name only "Student's t-test and one-sample t-test"; and the paper writes "paired" where it pairs ("2-tailed paired t-test", Fig. 2D legend) but not for Fig. 7B or in Results 3.7 ("two-sided t-test"). The criterion of fig7b-test-pulse-unchanged-to-760 now takes the unpaired two-sample two-sided t-test against the control group as the paper's test, shows the paired test and the one-sample test as variants, and drops the methods_underspecified rule. fig7b-test-pulse-reduced-1200-1400 now uses the same test, with only the pole readings left open, and fig7a-test-pulse-cosine-up-to-1000 refers to that test in place of its readings. Expected values and statements are unchanged.

    Why it matters. Raised by the round-2 verifier. Notebooks

Every registered entry that uses the paper’s datasets. An entry can hold several notebooks, and can use more than one dataset.

Reproduction Membrane charging and lesioning by pulsed electric fields (Semenov et al. 2026)

Tests the findings of Semenov et al. (2026) on dandiset 001515, which holds FluoVolt strobe-imaging time courses of CHO cells charged by single 1-us pulses at one amplitude per cell. None of the paper's experiments (sets of 40 baseline and 60 pulse images at 900 ns with field escalation, Di-8-ANEPPS, 0.3-us pulses, Gd3+, two-pulse and Ca2+ recordings) is in it, so 33 of 34 claims are blocked; the one computation from the paper's Eq. (2) is tested. The notebook checks what the files hold. All files

Environment: Python 3.13, 101 packages pinned in requirements.lock.txt; CI runs each notebook from a new environment built from it.

Reproduction Membrane charging and recovery after pulsed electric fields (Semenov et al. 2026)

Tests the findings of Semenov, Silkuniene, Silkunas, Pakhomova, Bixler, Ibey, Kochunov and Pakhomov (2026) on dandiset 001516. The dandiset holds a FluoVolt two-pulse experiment in CHO cells, with inter-pulse intervals of 1 to 250 us and fields of 150 to 1570 V/cm, which the paper does not report; none of the recordings behind the paper's figures is in it. The one claim that is a computation from the paper's Eq. (2) is tested, and the dataset's own sets are measured with the paper's pole method. All files

Reproduction Membrane charging and electroporation imaged with strobe fluorescence (Semenov et al. 2026)

Tests the findings of Semenov, Silkuniene, Silkunas, Pakhomova, Bixler, Ibey, Kochunov and Pakhomov (2026) on dandiset 001517, which holds raw strobe images of CHO cells loaded with FluoVolt or Di-8-ANEPPS and exposed to 1-us pulses of escalating amplitude. Pole polarization, its decline within and across sets, the dye calibration against the Schwan equation and the departure from linear charging are rebuilt from the images. The Gd3+, 0.3-us, two-pulse, alternating-amplitude and Ca2+ experiments are not in the dandiset. All files
