Reproduction Membrane charging measured with FluoVolt and Di-8-ANEPPS (Semenov et al. 2026)

Tests the findings of Semenov, Silkuniene, Silkunas, Pakhomova, Bixler, Ibey, Kochunov and Pakhomov (2026) on dandiset 001518, which holds ten escalations of 1-us pulses from 25 to 250 V, five with each dye, imaged by pulsed laser microscopy. It tests the calibration of the two dyes, the FluoVolt and Di-8-ANEPPS responses up to electroporating fields, and Eq. (2) of the paper. The files give the pulse in volts; the factor to V/cm is assumed from the electrode geometry (1400 V/cm at 250 V) and every criterion is also run over a range of factors. The alternating, 0.3-us, Gd3+, Ca2+ and two-pulse experiments are not in the dandiset. All files

Method differences

How the tests differed from the paper’s method or data, and the claims each applies to.

  • Field strengths are matched in order to the generator volts the files give (25 V as 43 V/cm, 50 as 150, 70 as 230, 100 as 360, 150 as 570, 200 as 780, 250 as 1000, 300 as 1200, 350 as 1400), an assumption. The paper names nine field strengths for its 1-us escalations and the files nine amplitudes, but the two are not proportional, and below electroporation the data fit a proportional response about as well on the volts as on the paper's V/cm. The first step supports the matching: from 25 to 50 V the mean pole |dF| triples (FluoVolt 3.05, Di-8-ANEPPS 2.90), against 3.49 for the paper's 43 to 150 V/cm and 2.00 for proportional volts. A variant with the field proportional to the volts (4 V/cm per V) is shown for every claim that uses the field strength as a number.fig1-pole-dimming fig2a-fluovolt-saturation fig2a-fluovolt-stable fig2b-di8-climbs-to-570 fig2b-di8-bleaching fig2b-di8-decline-within-set fig3-dye-sensitivity fig3-fluovolt-nonlinear fig4a-fluovolt-limits fig4a-fluovolt-cathodic-decline fig4b-di8-limits fig4b-first-10-pulses fig4b-cathodic-asymmetry fig4c-equator-follows-cosine fig7b-charging-during-pulse
  • Background is subtracted from ROIs 30 to 36 pixels (8 to 10 um) outside the membrane, 20 degrees wide, near each pole (the far reading), an assumption. The paper does not state a background method for the laser images; its Methods follow reference [20] ('Most methods were identical to those described in detail recently [20]'), which places background ROIs 'near the poles outside of the cell' with no distance. The data check shows the membrane's glow is still a third of its excess 2 um out and a few percent 8 um out. The near (2 to 4 um) and no-background readings are shown and change dF by up to a factor of three. After verification round 1 every claim also shows a sweep of 6-pixel bands from 8 to 44 pixels out and a per-pole (local) background.fig1-pole-dimming fig2a-fluovolt-saturation fig2a-fluovolt-stable fig2b-di8-climbs-to-570 fig2b-di8-bleaching fig2b-di8-decline-within-set fig3-dye-sensitivity fig3-fluovolt-nonlinear fig4a-fluovolt-limits fig4a-fluovolt-cathodic-decline fig4b-di8-limits fig4b-first-10-pulses fig4b-cathodic-asymmetry fig4c-equator-follows-cosine fig7b-charging-during-pulse
  • The perimeter trace (6 pixels wide, Methods 2.3) is found automatically as the peak of intensity on 360 rays of each set's baseline image, with polar angles from the cell centre; the paper drew it by hand in MetaMorph and resampled by perimeter length. Sets whose outline radius departs from the cell's median by more than 15% are traced again within 8 pixels of the cell's median centre and 4 pixels of its median radius; this re-traced FluoVolt c2 at 300 V (outline on a neighbouring cell) and Di-8-ANEPPS c7 at 300 and 350 V (outline on a body inside the cell), found in the outline overlays added after verification round 1.fig1-pole-dimming fig2a-fluovolt-saturation fig2a-fluovolt-stable fig2b-di8-climbs-to-570 fig2b-di8-bleaching fig2b-di8-decline-within-set fig3-dye-sensitivity fig3-fluovolt-nonlinear fig4a-fluovolt-limits fig4a-fluovolt-cathodic-decline fig4b-di8-limits fig4b-first-10-pulses fig4b-cathodic-asymmetry fig4c-equator-follows-cosine fig7b-charging-during-pulse
  • Frames without a laser flash, which the files do not flag (frame 0 of 137 of 141 sets and 290 others), are left out of every average; the paper does not mention them. Keeping them is shown as a variant.fig1-pole-dimming fig2a-fluovolt-saturation fig2a-fluovolt-stable fig2b-di8-climbs-to-570 fig2b-di8-bleaching fig2b-di8-decline-within-set fig3-dye-sensitivity fig3-fluovolt-nonlinear fig4a-fluovolt-limits fig4a-fluovolt-cathodic-decline fig4b-di8-limits fig4b-first-10-pulses fig4b-cathodic-asymmetry fig4c-equator-follows-cosine fig7b-charging-during-pulse
  • The field direction is not stored. The cathode-facing pole is placed where dF is positive (Methods 2.3, emission increased in the cathode-facing hemisphere), on one axis for all sets estimated from the 50 to 100 V sets (per-set median deviation 1.6 degrees).fig1-pole-dimming fig2a-fluovolt-saturation fig2a-fluovolt-stable fig2b-di8-climbs-to-570 fig2b-di8-bleaching fig2b-di8-decline-within-set fig3-dye-sensitivity fig3-fluovolt-nonlinear fig4a-fluovolt-limits fig4a-fluovolt-cathodic-decline fig4b-di8-limits fig4b-first-10-pulses fig4b-cathodic-asymmetry fig4c-equator-follows-cosine fig7b-charging-during-pulse
  • Factor, cell, trace, pixel and background choices as in fig3-dye-sensitivity.fig2a-fluovolt-saturation fig2a-fluovolt-stable fig4a-fluovolt-limits fig4a-fluovolt-cathodic-decline fig2b-di8-climbs-to-570 fig2b-di8-decline-within-set fig4b-di8-limits fig4b-first-10-pulses fig4b-cathodic-asymmetry fig4c-equator-follows-cosine fig1-pole-dimming fig7b-charging-during-pulse
  • All eight Di-8-ANEPPS escalation cells (c7 to c14) are used where the paper reports n = 5 without naming them; the criterion is also run on each of the 56 five-cell subsets.fig1-pole-dimming fig2b-di8-climbs-to-570 fig2b-di8-bleaching fig2b-di8-decline-within-set fig3-dye-sensitivity fig3-fluovolt-nonlinear fig4b-di8-limits fig4b-first-10-pulses fig4b-cathodic-asymmetry fig4c-equator-follows-cosine fig7b-charging-during-pulse
  • Di-8-ANEPPS c13 has two 150 V sets; the first, in escalation order, is used.fig1-pole-dimming fig2b-di8-climbs-to-570 fig2b-di8-bleaching fig2b-di8-decline-within-set fig4b-di8-limits fig4b-first-10-pulses fig4b-cathodic-asymmetry fig4c-equator-follows-cosine fig7b-charging-during-pulse
  • FluoVolt cells c2, c3, c4 and c6 are used, the four with exactly the nine-set escalation of Fig. 2A (n = 4); c5, whose escalation follows an extra 350 V set, is a variant.fig2a-fluovolt-saturation fig2a-fluovolt-stable fig3-dye-sensitivity fig3-fluovolt-nonlinear fig4a-fluovolt-limits fig4a-fluovolt-cathodic-decline
  • The pixel size is taken as 0.267 um (16-um pixels of the iXon Ultra 897 behind the 60x objective, Methods 2.3); the files do not give it. Mean radii are then 7.8 to 15.1 um by cell (perimeter 7.84 to 15.06, equal-area 7.80 to 14.92; 7.84 to 12.85 over the cells used) against the paper's average of about 8 um; a variant uses the 0.192-um pixel that gives 8 um.fig3-dye-sensitivity fig3-fluovolt-nonlinear fig4a-fluovolt-limits fig4b-di8-limits fig4c-equator-follows-cosine
  • The charging time constant is 44.5 ns per um of radius, the fit of reference [20] Fig. 5C, as Methods 2.3 says.fig3-dye-sensitivity fig3-fluovolt-nonlinear fig4a-fluovolt-limits fig4b-di8-limits fig4c-equator-follows-cosine
  • The files give generator voltages and the paper field strengths, with no conversion in either. Two readings are run. M1, 3.9 V/cm per V, is adopted as the main reading (an assumption, as the paper gives no conversion), because the single-pulse voltages then fall on the paper's field series 570 to 1400 V/cm and the six train voltages nearest the fields labelled in Fig. 6B (230, 570, 650, 780, 900, 1000 V/cm) have group sizes close to the n the figure gives (28, 38, 36, 68, 47, 39 against 28, 37, 34, 54, 35, 36). M2, 3.3 V/cm per V, puts the train voltages 70, 200 and 230 V on the 230, 650 and 780 V/cm named in Section 3.6 and is shown as a variant. A named field is assigned to the nearest voltage. Neither factor follows from the electrode gap (V/d is 10 V/cm per V for the 1.00 mm of the files, 6.25 for the 1.6 mm of Methods 2.2). The outcome is the same under both.fig6a-ca-entry-1000-1200 fig6a-ca-sustained fig6a-780-not-significant fig6b-train-threshold fig6b-650-weak-but-significant
  • Cells are found by an Otsu threshold and watershed of the pre-pulse mean image, with a circular region of 0.7 of each cell's equivalent radius and the median of pixels more than 15 px from any cell as background; the paper drew the regions by hand (Methods 2.4) and these are standard choices, shown with regions of 0.5 and 1.0 of the radius, end windows of 5 and 20 frames, and a background taken as the 10th percentile of the cell-free pixels.fig6a-ca-entry-1000-1200 fig6a-ca-sustained fig6a-780-not-significant fig6b-train-threshold fig6b-650-weak-but-significant
  • Background subtraction is an assumption. Section 2.3 describes dF = 100 (F - F0)/F0 with no background step, section 2.4 subtracts the background fluorescence of a cell-free region for Fluo-4, and the claims file names both readings. The adopted reading subtracts, for 2025-05-01, the mean of the cell-free background recording made with each cell (the files call it a recording in the background region without cells, so the experimenters recorded it with every cell of that session), point by point along the trace; for 2025-05-06, which has no background recordings, the per-image median of a cell-free annulus 2 to 3 radii from the cell, whose level the background recordings of the first session match within 0.97 to 1.08 times. Cell-free background is 52 to 54% of the trace intensity, and 65 to 66 counts above the darkest pixels (no dark frame or camera offset is given), and the paper's own values at 1000 V/cm (40 and 26.6%) are on the scale of the subtracted readings and about 3 times the unsubtracted ones (40/13.3 = 3.0 and 26.6/8.5 = 3.1 under window (b)). The notebook shows every reading (background recording, annulus, darkest-pixel floor, none). Round 1 adopted the annulus for both sessions; the background recording was adopted in the revision after round 1, after the results under both readings had been seen, and in that revision the annulus reading stopped meeting fig2c-1000-within-set while the background recording met it. The choice is to be read as made after the results.fig2cd-nonelectroporating-stable fig2c-1000-within-set fig2d-lasting-reduction fig4d-short-pulses-same-limit
  • The analysed cell is the one nearest the image centre of the cell's sham recording (an assumption; the files do not say which of the one to eight cells found in each field was analysed, and section 2.2 centres the field of view between the electrodes). The notebook draws every traced membrane on its baseline image.fig2cd-nonelectroporating-stable fig2c-1000-within-set fig2d-lasting-reduction fig4d-short-pulses-same-limit
  • The 6-pixel-wide perimeter trace is found automatically as the outer ridge of the membrane staining in the sham recording's baseline image and moved by the measured drift (at most 2 pixels), where the paper draws it by hand in MetaMorph (section 2.3); the 360 points are spaced by 1 degree of angle, not by equal arc length. For 2025-05-01 c4 the default rule (steepest outward fall) put the trace on internal staining (median radius 21.2 px), so that cell is traced with the outermost strong fall (34.1 px); the other cells keep the default rule, which the overlays show on the membrane (c3's trace cuts inside the cell on one side, away from the poles).fig2cd-nonelectroporating-stable fig2c-1000-within-set fig2d-lasting-reduction fig4d-short-pulses-same-limit
  • The cathodic pole angle is the median over the session's cells of the phase of a cosine fitted to the polarization profile at non-electroporating fields (83.0 degrees for 1 us, 82.1 degrees for 0.3 us, close to straight up, as in Fig. 1); the paper places the poles by the field direction. Per-cell phases (73.1 to 93.6 degrees) are shown as a variant.fig2cd-nonelectroporating-stable fig2c-1000-within-set fig2d-lasting-reduction fig4d-short-pulses-same-limit
  • Field strengths are the paper's labels (Fig. 2C; the Fig. 4D axis labels 357, 760, 1000, 1200, 1420, 1630, 2050, 2473 and 3320 V/cm, read from the figure on page 6 of the PDF) matched by order to the pulse amplitudes in volts that the files give; the labels are not proportional to the volts.fig2cd-nonelectroporating-stable fig2c-1000-within-set fig2d-lasting-reduction fig4d-short-pulses-same-limit
  • Every detected cell in every file is used, as the dataset does not say which cells the paper used.fig6a-ca-entry-1000-1200 fig6a-ca-sustained fig6a-780-not-significant
  • Same trace, cell, field direction, strength mapping, benchmark, cell count and main reading as fig7a-test-pulse-cosine-up-to-1000.fig7b-test-pulse-unchanged-to-760 fig7b-test-pulse-reduced-1200-1400 fig7c-full-reseal-mild-residual-strong
  • 11 cells are used where the paper reports n = 8; the dataset does not say which 8. Four cells depart from the described order of sets (c3 and c4 before the 1000 V/cm exposure, c1 and c11 after); sets are matched by their rank at each field. The notebook applies the criteria without c4, without c3 and c4, without all four, and to every choice of 8 of the 11 cells.fig2cd-nonelectroporating-stable fig2c-1000-within-set fig2d-lasting-reduction
  • None in method; Eq. (2) and its constants are those of Methods 2.3, and both readings of the criterion were run.fig5b-conductivity-calibration
  • The first lit image stands for the first image where frame 0 has no flash.fig2a-fluovolt-stable
  • None; the computation uses the equation and constants of Methods 2.3 and no recordings.fig5b-conductivity-calibration
  • The pulse is given in volts; the factor to V/cm is assumed to be 5.6 V/cm per V (1400 V/cm at 250 V, the top mapping). A two-dimensional model of the electrodes of Methods 2.2 (rods 0.5 mm in diameter, 1.6 mm apart) gives 4.64 to 7.19 V/cm per V at the midpoint, depending on whether 1.6 mm is the gap or the centre distance. The bottom mapping (43 V/cm at 25 V, 1.72 V/cm per V) matches the paper's dye sensitivities, which fig3-dye-sensitivity tests, and is run as a variant. Every criterion is also run over factors from 1.5 to 8 V/cm per V.fig3-dye-sensitivity
  • Each field of view holds 3 to 6 cells; the paper used one cell per experiment and does not say which. Experiment means (adopted), the cell nearest the image centre and all cells are run.fig3-dye-sensitivity
  • The membrane trace is found automatically (peak of the baseline image along rays from the cell centre, 6 samples 1 pixel apart), not drawn by hand; 360 points at equal angles stand for the resampling along the perimeter (Methods 2.3). In some cells the trace is drawn inwards by bright spots inside the cell, and one object in c7 is two touching cells; a trace that follows the cell edge is run as a variant over every criterion and changes no result at the adopted factor.fig3-dye-sensitivity
  • Pixel size 0.267 um (16-um camera pixels over the 60x objective of Methods 2.3) is assumed; the files give none. The mean radius is 6.7 um with the peak trace and 9.9 um with the edge trace, against the about 8 um the paper uses in Eq. (2).fig3-dye-sensitivity
  • Frame 0 and other dark frames (laser misfires) are left out, so most baselines have 39 images.fig3-dye-sensitivity
  • Background subtraction (median of cell-free pixels of the same image) is adopted; the paper states it only for Ca2+ imaging (Methods 2.4). No subtraction and the camera offset alone are run as variants.fig3-dye-sensitivity
  • Points, line and readings as in fig3-dye-sensitivity (adopted reading); LOWESS with delta of 1% of the IMPC range, a standard speed-up.fig3-fluovolt-nonlinear
  • The fit through the origin keeps the uniform bleaching offset of each profile, as the paper reports bleaching in its own Di-8-ANEPPS dF (Results 3.2, paragraph 3); removing it is run as a variant.fig3-fluovolt-nonlinear
  • The mean of per-image dF over the pulse images equals the collapsed-image dF, as both use the same F0; the Fig. 2 and Fig. 4 measures coincide.fig2a-fluovolt-saturation
  • FluoVolt n is 5 experiments here, against 4 in the paper.fig2a-fluovolt-saturation
  • The sets are those nearest the paper's levels; all 19 sets are shown as a variant.fig2a-fluovolt-stable
  • Under the top mapping the 43 V/cm level has no set, so the steps are tested from 150 V/cm.fig2b-di8-climbs-to-570
  • The text does not name the pole; reading (c), the cathodic pole, is assumed, as the paper places the damage at the cathodic pole (Results 3.1, the cathodic pole dims at 780 V/cm; Results 3.4, the reduction is more pronounced at the cathodic pole). Reading (d) is shown.fig2b-di8-decline-within-set
  • Figure 1's cell is not identified; the Di-8-ANEPPS escalation cells are used, as the criterion says.fig1-pole-dimming
  • The 560 and 760 V/cm of Results 3.7 are the 570 and 780 V/cm sets (claims file header).fig7b-charging-during-pulse
  • The paper names no test for this panel; the claims file's readings (one-sample t-test or Student's t-test against 0 V/cm, end value or peak) are all run.fig6a-ca-entry-1000-1200
  • The cells were tested more than once in the dataset; all exposures are used, and the first exposure of each field of view is shown as a variant.fig6b-train-threshold
  • Reading (b), above 3 s.d. of the reference group, is run as above its mean plus 3 s.d. and, literally, above 3 s.d.fig6b-train-threshold
  • The perimeter trace is found automatically, where the paper draws "the 6-pixel-wide trace ... along the cell perimeter" by hand in MetaMorph (Methods 2.3). The membrane at each angle is the intensity peak within 8 px inside the cell's outer edge (the steepest outward fall of intensity), an assumption adopted after the first round's plain intensity peak was seen to fall inside two cells; that trace is shown as a variant, and so are windows of 4, 6, 10 and 12 px.fig7a-test-pulse-cosine-up-to-1000
  • The analysed cell is the largest object in each subject's field (an assumption; the paper says one cell per experiment, Fig. 2 legend, and does not say how it was chosen).fig7a-test-pulse-cosine-up-to-1000
  • The field direction in image coordinates is not given by the dataset; it is the A-weighted circular mean of cosine-fit phases over all 5-us files (-75.6 degrees, per-file sd 6.8), and from the control files alone as a variant (-73.9 degrees). 0 degrees is the depolarized pole, as Fig. 7A plots it.fig7a-test-pulse-cosine-up-to-1000
  • Conditioning strengths are mapped by rank from the files' generator volts (100 to 350 V) to the Fig. 7A labels 360 to 1400 V/cm, and the single 50 V pulse, recorded first in every cell, is taken as the 150/150 benchmark; an assumption, as the files give no field strengths and no 150/150 pair. Read as steps of 100 V from 0.15 kV/cm at 50 V, the strengths in the files' general description would put the levels 0 to 11% lower, with the same order.fig7a-test-pulse-cosine-up-to-1000
  • Six 5-us cells are in the dataset where the paper reports n = 5; all six are used, and each five-cell subset is shown.fig7a-test-pulse-cosine-up-to-1000
  • Background and dark frames are open in the paper. Main reading adopted as an assumption, background removed (median of an annulus 1.6 to 2.2 radii) and frames without a laser flash dropped; the paper's own scale (Fig. 7A about 13% dF at 150/150; 9.1% dF per 100 mV in Results 3.3, about 16% dF at 150 V/cm) is met only with a background removed (12.6 to 12.9% against 5.6%), and the files' camera floor is about 209 counts under a membrane trace of 451 to 692. All four combinations are shown.fig7a-test-pulse-cosine-up-to-1000
  • The criterion's pole dF is tested under the pole readings of fig7b-test-pulse-unchanged-to-760 (cathodic, anodic, mean of both), with its test, the unpaired two-sample t-test against the control group (the paired test shown as a variant); reading (a) compares the fit at each pole with the s.e. of the pole mean over cells, for the legend's gray outline.fig7a-test-pulse-cosine-up-to-1000
  • Normalized test dF for the pole comparison is per-cell dF over the group control of each pole; normalization by each cell's own control is shown as a variant. A standard choice the criterion leaves open; it does not change the result at 1400 V/cm.fig7c-full-reseal-mild-residual-strong
  • The fifth pre-exposure 150 V/cm set exists in one cell only and is not tested.fig2cd-nonelectroporating-stable
  • The third post-exposure 150 V/cm set exists in 2 cells and is not tested.fig2d-lasting-reduction
  • 8 cells are used where the paper reports n = 10; one cell's 450 V set has no Fig. 4D field and is left out, and two cells lack the 100 V (357 V/cm) set and one the 800 V (3320 V/cm) set.fig4d-short-pulses-same-limit
  • Eq. (2) is read from the PDF, as the extracted text garbles it.fig5b-eq2-sensitivity

Outcomes describe what one attempt could reproduce from the data in the dataset, with the methods available. A failure to reproduce may be a failure of the agent, and is not a judgement of the paper.
